Ladies and gentlemen, nobody can escape that the deterioration of rural education is walking in parallel to the dismantling process that is suffering from the whole of rural life. The Socialist Group believes that this issue should be integrated into a more global rural development project in all its facets, and that is why we are always talking about the need for proper treatment of positive discrimination and planning beyond a single councillor, since education alone cannot address a problem like this. It would be a question, ladies and gentlemen, of bringing forward a global project based on rational occupation of the territory in order to achieve rural populations, and that is why we say that education cannot do it alone. It would basically be to achieve a social, territorial, educational, cultural and economic balance that would anticipate the vital need for decent survival of rural people and their people to the mere economic and short-term issues in order to achieve education with the same quality parameters as those enjoyed by people living in cities. And I'm going to put just a few examples in which the Socialist Group has been very insistent in recent times: we have to be able to eliminate the digital divide between the city centers and the rural centers; we have to be who, too, to prevent children from our rural schools from receiving their classes at levels that correspond to their age and course, and not to make completely anti-pedagogic groupings that clearly harm the service they receive. We must also be able to guarantee the same quality of education with the necessary staff and with decent educational equipment and we must be able to ensure that complementary services for school transport and dining also serve to achieve this important objective. By the way, Counsellor, we have two problems there in Vilagarcia and Cuntis, with the problem of companies that are providing catering to the dining rooms. Please do something about this. I'll get back to it. I also want to emphasize the fundamental: in view of the primacy of the short-term economic criterion that deepens in the territorial imbalance, the rights of children to receive educational attention in their midst must be given to them during the early stages as a way to dignify the rural world and avoid their depopulation. Ladies and gentlemen, there will be no development in rural areas if there is no change in the attitudes of the entire population, and for this reason, recognition and respect, esteem and knowledge and values of rural areas should be present in some way in the entire educational activity of the country. A question that must affect not only the few unitary schools that are maintained, but also the schools, the ICC, and the institutes that have a student who mostly comes from the rural area. Without protecting our agricultural, forestry sector, without essential public services of education, health and care for older people, it will be very difficult to maintain the rural population. If we don't provide rural schools with the necessary human and material resources for quality education – including children's, primary and support staff specialists – it will be difficult for families to bet on living in rural areas. And if we do not facilitate the use of basic supplementary services for school transport and dining, it will also be very difficult for families to bet on staying in rural areas, because school transport plays a very important role in facilitating the school stage, not only in compulsory education, Counsellor, but also in undergraduate and vocational training, because it is the only means they have to be able to reach their reference institute, because there are no other ordinary public transport. Finally, ladies and gentlemen, the Socialist Group will therefore support this initiative which is being presented in this House today in defence of positive discrimination in favour of rural education. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-11-10-DSPG128.u268	Mr. President. Good afternoon, gentlemen. We bring back to the plenary of this institution a subject that has already come four times on the initiative of the Socialist Parliamentary Group. At the initiative of the Socialist Parliamentary Group, the Commission was three times the second time. I understand it's a subject that you can see as a tedious and difficult question to understand, but I'm going to try to insist on it and explain it in case there's still someone left with some doubt, or some inability to understand what's going on. The subject of what we are talking about, which the last time it was discussed was through an interpellation of the Socialist Parliamentary Group in this House just 14 days ago, on October 27, it refers to the abolition of the extension of the renewal of the grant subsidies granted under the national housing plans 2002-2005, 2005-2008 and 2009-2012. I used to say before I understand that it's a lousy subject because of its difficulty, and it's a subject that neither wakes up the social sensitivity – for now – that it should, since this withdrawal of these aids, the suppression of these extensions, is masked by a fact that is coincident with time, which is the fact that the practice of all these loans from these families that acquired their home based on these public aids did so through contracts, from mortgages, with a variable interest referred to the euribor. And the euribor, ladies and gentlemen, is at 0.14% today. And it also coincides with the deletion of ground clauses. In such a way that, at many times, even families who lose this subsidy, an amount of 0.14% to an amount of EUR 80,000 means to pay 10 euros per month of interest. That's why, today on this subject, there may not be sensitivity even if there should be. But, ladies and gentlemen, I must remind you that as a legal imperative, as a result of these national housing plans, all those who were benefited from these aid had to assume a number of commitments or obligations. One very important, for the purposes of what we're dealing with, is that the credit had to be subscribed for a minimum period of twenty-five years; I repeat, a minimum period of twenty-five years. And secondly, that there were such depreciation limitations that, for example, a loan of EUR 90,000 in 10 years, at most, could amortize 8,000; and if more than 8,000 is amortized, it is at risk of losing the aid and subsidies granted so far. One of the conditions of these aid, ladies and gentlemen, is that these aid was granted for periods of five years, and they were automatically extended for five more years, for periods of five years, whenever the family units continued to meet the economic requirements. In the regulation itself, it is established that this five-year concession is extendable for periods of equal duration, for the sole purpose, as indicated in the above-mentioned housing plans, to be updated in relation to the economic variations produced in the family unit would benefit from aid. Well, ladies and gentlemen, in 2012, through a decree, Decree 20/2012, in its article 35, the Government of the nation decided to abolish all aid that was in the processing period, and hence no longer granted. Ladies and gentlemen, the true expectation – because this would be a great challenge for families – but the real expectation is that later on, through an interpretative circular of the Ministry of Development, it is apparently subscribed by a general direction of this Ministry of Development but not signed by anyone, this abolition applies not only to the National Plan 2009-2012, as was said in the decree, but to all the previous ones and also to the extensions of the aid already granted. It is such a disaster and so overrun and so lack of legal basis this action that the Government of the People's Party itself has been obliged, through the Popular Parliamentary Group, to implement an amendment to a different law, which was the Law on the flexibility of the rental market – therefore, it was not a law of acquiring housing – by means of a second additional provision which, in some way, gives legal protection to all these measures. In other words, the second additional provision provides that no new aid will be granted and provides that the aid granted in the previous plans will not be extended. From that point on, and beyond the problem that arises, which is a genuine violation of constitutional law, a genuine legal scourge, which is that the laws have no retroactive effects but on those issues that are favourable to the citizen himself, that basic principle which is the irretroactivity of the norm and which is completely violated, apart from all this, a problem arises, gentlemen, that will have grave transcendence for the future. Today we're talking here and now with a 0.14% euribor, but, ladies and gentlemen, six years ago the euribor was at 4%, and within six years, how will the euribor be? We don't know. And within ten, how will the euribor be? We don't know, we don't even know how the euribor will be in a year. What we do know, ladies and gentlemen, is that a loan of €90,000 to €0.14% pays 10 euros per month of interest, but a credit of €90,000 to €4% or 5% or 6% would pay €450 or 300 per month to interest. Ladies and gentlemen, we are talking about families with little economic resources. We took into account that in the national housing plans 2002-2005 and 2005-2008, the income top per family unit was twice and half the IPREM; that is, we can be talking about 1,500 euros a month. It is true that this peak in Plan 2009-2012 has been raised four times the IPREM, but most of the families we are talking about are families with annual incomes of less than 15,000 euros, and these families are those that are being affected and those that can be seriously damaged at a given time when there is a rise in interest rates. Then there will surely be many who are surprised. We, ladies and gentlemen, have to say that our group has brought this Parliament seven times, four times this plenary session and three times the corresponding committee, this problem. Therefore, the proposal that we bring here is that "the Galician Parliament urges the Government of Galicia to first authorize all the requested extensions of the aid for the grant of loans granted to the landfill of the national housing plans 2002-2005, 2005-2008 and 2009-2012, formulated from 5 June 2013, which comply with the requirements set out in those plans." Second, that "the Galician Council and the Galician Parliament ask the Government of Spain to leave without effect the second additional provision of Law 4/2013, of 4 June, of measures for the flexibility and promotion of the housing rental market, as well as of Article 35 of Royal Decree/2012, as regards aid previously granted and to comply with the obligations arising from those plans". Thirdly, we ask you, ladies and gentlemen, that "subsidiarily to the former, as long as this problem is not solved by the absolute majority of the People's Party in the State Parliament, by the Council of Galicia, as the administration which holds the powers in the field of housing, sole administration and administration which has dealt with these aids subsidiaryly, if you take charge of these aid without prejudice to their impact on the General Administration of the State". For my part, nothing else, thank you very much.

It is a project created and promoted by the Bipartisan Government Environmental Council with a clear objective: to be an instrument of environmental awareness to re-evaluate our natural spaces, and to bring to society the importance of protection figures such as the Natura 2000 Network. Because it's not going to be that we keep feeding centers of great characteristics, of great dimensions, for those who then, in the end, don't have enough resources to keep them. I was talking here about the partner of the Joint Group of a study by the University of Coruña that says that there are 188 interpretation centers in Galicia, and that, from these centers, a very important part was built with European funds. Because there was a requirement in the European funds, and it was that it was easier to access those resources if there were really interpretation centres. But it's just, when these kind of centers were built, they didn't think about maintenance, staff, services, and many of them are abandoned. We understand that a solution must be given to that building. We understand that it's logical that this isn't like a skeleton that's just a lump of cement, a metaphor more than a mismanagement policy of public resources. And we also believe that we have to question that macrocentre model, that model that, in a way, even in a much smaller dimension, reminds us of something that we have very close to this institution in which we are in Santiago de Compostela today. So we are not going to oppose this initiative, but we also believe that we need to give some rationality and a little more thorough debate about whether these should at this time be the priorities we have in the field of the environment, because sometimes we confuse the means with the ends.
